The landing redesign is built and working on `feat/landing-redesign`. Nothing is committed, and no PR is open until you ask.

**On the hero panel you flagged:** it now sits under the text at full width (up to 1080px), instead of beside it. Emails and changed values fit on one line, and the four counts get more room. On phones the counts go under their labels, so "Only vendor says same" no longer cuts off its number.

**The new page, top to bottom:**
- **Nav:** a floating bar with links to each section; the one you're in is highlighted, and a thin coral line along the top shows how far down you are. It also has the theme menu, Sign in and "Try the demo".
- **Hero:** a results panel built from the demo's real recorded run. You can switch between Northwind Match and Clearline Resolve and move the match cut-off. The scores, the four counts and each pair's label update using the same calculations as the Results screen. Clicking a pair shows both records with the differences highlighted, Jev's score and what the vendor said.
- **How it works:** on wide screens it holds in place while you scroll and follows one real pair, Jane Smith and Jane Smyth (a duplicate both vendors missed), from files to rules to Jev's verdict. On phones, or with reduced motion, it's a plain list.
- **Other sections:**
  - A closer look, as three cards.
  - Who it's for.
  - What it measures, with a new "Your data" point. Its claims are checked against the code: Jev never sees record IDs, vendor names or vendor decisions, and deleting an evaluation deletes its records.
  - A five-question FAQ.
  - The closing panel, kept dark in both themes.
  - A four-column footer.
- **Motion:** scroll animation uses Motion, and smooth wheel scrolling uses Lenis, the one new dependency. Section headings fill in word by word as you scroll. The old blur, drift, parallax and sheen effects are removed.

**Checks:**
- Lint, typecheck, format and the production build all pass.
- The copy test now passes after I reworded one FAQ question.
- The public e2e suite passes: 13 tests, including two new ones covering the vendor switch, the cut-off (counts checked against the real data), opening a pair, the FAQ and the section links. Accessibility checks pass in light and dark.
- No sideways scrolling at 1440px or 375px, in either theme.
- I changed the existing theme test to wait for the page to finish loading before reopening the theme menu. The landing now ships more JavaScript, so its first click landed before the menu was ready; the check that the theme applies before the page paints is unchanged.
- 10 unit tests fail, all in the standards check, because it can't start ESLint on Windows. They fail the same way without my changes, so they're specific to this machine.

**Still to do before a PR:** the full e2e run (including the "Try the demo" sandbox test, which needs the PairSight e2e setup), the complete `/local-review`, and a final pull of `main`.

**From the mockup, changed on purpose:**
- Its fonts and colours are replaced by ours.
- Its invented names and numbers are replaced by the demo's real data.
- Its "Your data" placeholder now holds facts checked against the code.
- The panel no longer scales as you scroll, to keep the motion calm.

Have a look on :3000 and tell me what you'd like changed.

You understood it right: the pair suggestions only work on people evaluations today.

**Where the code limits it:**
- `src/server/pairsight/splink/score-dataset.ts:139` skips the job when the evaluation's type isn't `person`, and records the reason as `not_people`.
- The scoring model in `services/splink/pairsight_splink/model.py` is built only for people. It compares first name, surname, date of birth, postcode, email and phone. It also narrows the search for people only: same-sounding surname plus birth year, same-sounding first name plus postcode area, or exactly the same postcode.

**What organisations and products get instead:**
- They start with their type's default comparison rules (the hand-written TypeScript ones), so the plan is never empty.
- People evaluations with fewer than 200 records also get the default rules, because the model needs enough records to learn from.
- If the scoring isn't set up or fails, the plan falls back to the default rules too, and says so.

The roadmap (`docs/PairSight-Scale-Roadmap.md`, item 6) leaves removing the TypeScript rules for later, "organisations and products still do" need them.

**What it would take to extend this:** a separate model for each type, since organisations need comparisons like company name, registration number and address, and products need things like SKU and description. Each new model would be benchmarked first, as the Splink service rule requires.

The first PR, which gives organisations and products their own pair suggestions, is built. The full e2e suite hasn't finished: Claude Code stopped it, together with the :3100 test server and the Jev stub, because the machine ran critically low on memory. I haven't restarted them; say if you'd like me to.

Nothing is committed yet. The landing redesign is safe on `feat/ui-revamp` (3 commits, not pushed), and this work is on `feat/suggestions-all-types`.

**What changed**
- The suggestion service has one model per type: people (unchanged), organisations and products.
- **Organisations** compare the name with legal endings ignored, company number, tax ID, website, email, phone and address. They also catch a firm filed under its trading name.
- **Products** compare barcode, part number, SKU, name, brand and manufacturer. A different size or colour counts strongly against a match, because Jev treats it as a different product.
- **Two training fixes that apply to every type:**
  - **Company numbers, barcodes, sizes and colours have fixed weights.** Left to learn them, it learned that a different company number means nothing.
  - **No single difference can veto a match.** On 2,000-record files, one difference could outweigh everything else.
- **On screen:** text that only talked about people now names each type's fields, and there are new plain-English reasons such as "the same company number" and "the same barcode".
- Your existing organisation and product evaluations that had been skipped for not being people are queued again.
- "Public Limited Company" and "PLC" now count as the same name.

**Benchmark against the default rules** (synthetic data with planted duplicates, at the default cut-off):

| | 2K records | 10K | 100K |
|---|---|---|---|
| People | 78% vs 66% | 91% vs 71% | 90% vs 70% |
| Organisations | 79% vs 63% | 80% vs 67% | 89% vs 67% |
| Products | 99% vs 78% | 98% vs 76% | 95% vs 76% |

Each cell is the share of planted duplicates found, PairSight vs the default rules, with no more pairs to check than the rules. Products also check about a fifth of the rules' pairs, and far fewer product variants get flagged as possible duplicates (228 vs 5,443 at 10K).

The weakest case is an organisation that moved city and changed website, with only its name in common. The rules caught 4 of 110 of those; Splink catches almost none at the default cut-off. Full tables are in the roadmap as item 10.

**Checks**
- Python lint and tests pass (26).
- Lint, typecheck, format, the secret check and the production build pass.
- 347 unit tests pass. The 10 failures are the same standards checks that fail on `main` on this machine.
- The three suggestion e2e tests pass: people, and new ones for organisations and products using 2,000-record fictional files.
- I deleted the test accounts' AI usage rows: 7,295, more than these tests alone made, so earlier runs' rows were still there. Only test-account rows were touched.

When you've tried it on :3000 (any organisation or product file of 200+ records), tell me whether to commit it as its own PR or go straight on to the second part. That part removes the rules, the switch and the Advanced section, and rebuilds the demos at about 2,000 records. I'll show you the pair counts before spending any Jev credits on the demos.

The new organisation and product test files are in `Downloads\PairSight - Datasets`, next to the people sets:

| Set | Records | Planted duplicates | Aggressive vendor | Careful vendor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `organisations-2500` | 2,500 | 206 | 794 pairs | 156 pairs |
| `organisations-10k` (also .xlsx) | 10,000 | 925 | 2,880 pairs | 709 pairs |
| `products-2500` | 2,500 | 219 | 807 pairs | 175 pairs |
| `products-10k` (also .xlsx) | 10,000 | 916 | 2,858 pairs | 678 pairs |

The sizes match what you said real files usually are (2,500), plus the app's 10,000-record limit for scale.

**What's in them**
- About 1 record in 10 has a planted duplicate, and about 4 in 10 of those are hard:
  - **Organisations:** a typo in the name, "Ltd" vs "Limited" or "&" vs "and", a firm filed under its trading name, or a firm that moved and changed website.
  - **Products:** a retyped name, no barcode, a part number written differently, or a brand written another way.
- Products come in families: the same line in another size or colour, which is a different product and shouldn't be suggested as a duplicate.
- **The careful vendor** finds about 70% of the duplicates and makes few wrong merges.
- **The aggressive vendor** finds about 95% of them. Most of its wrong merges are realistic look-alikes rather than random pairs: firms sharing a name, and a product's other sizes and colours.

**What to expect**
- PairSight should suggest roughly 200–300 pairs for a 2,500-record file and 900–1,200 for 10,000.
- It should find about 80% of the planted organisation duplicates and 95% or more of the product ones.
- The Rules card fills in a few seconds after the upload.

I updated the folder's `README.txt` with the sets and today's limits. I extended the existing `pnpm scale:datasets` script to make these, so the same command regenerates every file the same way. The people files were rewritten byte-for-byte unchanged.

Use the local stub rather than the real Jev key for the 10K sets: every suggested pair and every vendor pair is a Jev check. The :3100 test server and the stub are still stopped from the low-memory event, but on your dev server on :3000 the suggestions will run with the new models.

- Python tests (31), typecheck, lint, format, the copy check, the secret check and the database checks (RLS, no schema drift) all pass.
- 322 unit tests pass; the 10 failures are the same Windows-only standards checks as on `main`.
- **Docs:** updated across the product docs, rules and README, and the roadmap gets item 11.

Question, does the training we did with splink, keeps on learning? or its just as we did?


[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=9 session=e83fc832-7425-43cd-8ced-50de50186402]
timestamp: 2026-10-07T17:37:01.527Z
time: 2026-10-07 17:37
chars: 1625

No, it doesn't keep learning. It learns afresh from each file, then forgets.

**What happens on each upload:** when a records file is saved, Splink trains a new model on that file alone. With no labels, it works out how often real duplicates and unrelated records agree on each field: name, email, company number, barcode and so on. It then scores the pairs and stores the results for that dataset. A 2,000-person file therefore gets weights fitted to that file's own patterns. In one file a shared surname means little, because everyone's a Smith; in another it means more.

**What doesn't happen:**
- **Nothing carries over between files or customers.** No model is saved; each dataset starts from the same starting point.
- **It doesn't learn from Jev's answers.** Jev judges every suggested pair, but those verdicts never go back into Splink. The roadmap lists that as "left for later" (item 6): Jev's answers could sharpen the next run's suggestions.
- **The same file always gives the same pairs.** Training is seeded, which keeps runs reproducible and lets earlier Jev scores be reused.

**What we tuned is fixed code:** the comparisons per type, the fields with fixed weights (company number, barcode, size, colour), the 5% floor, and the fallback when nothing anchors the estimate. All of it lives in `services/splink/` and only changes when we change it and rerun the benchmark.

If you'd like it to improve over time, the natural next step is feeding Jev's verdicts back in as labels, either per evaluation (a second run gets sharper) or pooled across evaluations of the same type. That's a separate piece of work.

I've finished exploring and haven't changed anything. Here's how the two references map onto PairSight, then what I need from you before the plan.

## What ClickHouse's system is
- **Brand colour:** one colour in exactly three variants: **primary**, **primary-active** (pressed or hover, a little darker) and **primary-disabled** (a dull, low-contrast version). It covers every primary button, big stat number, link, input focus border, "featured" card and full-colour CTA band.
- **Everything else is neutral:** near-black canvas, then soft, card and elevated surfaces, hairline borders, and a four-step text scale (ink, body, muted, muted-soft). The only other colours are a few accents for status, which in our case are the category and chart colours.
- **Depth:** no shadows. It comes from slightly lighter surfaces plus 1px hairlines.
- **Type:** confident display weights (700 with tight negative tracking), 600 for titles and buttons, mono for data. Stat numbers are large and in the brand colour.
- **Shape:** 8px buttons and inputs, 12px cards, pill shapes only for badges. 96px between landing sections, a ~1280px container.
- **The mockup applies this to our app:** a 256px sidebar with numbered steps and a coral edge on the current one, and a solid top bar. Results pages open with a one-line verdict ("Atlas Resolve agreed with Jev on 9 of 13 pairs"), a cut-off bar, and two head-to-head vendor cards (big overall score, two sub-scores with tracks, a 4-part category bar). Sticky action bars at the bottom, mono uppercase eyebrows, `hero-num` stats.

## What that means for us
- **Brand variants: from 15 tokens down to 3.** Today we have `action`, `action-hover`, `action-ink`, `accent`, `accent-ink`, `ring`, `brand-deep/orange/amber`, the three `headline-*` gradient stops, the glows and `chart-5`. `accent-ink` alone is used in 25 files for links and active text. With three variants, links and active states move to the brand colour, and the gradient headline text and glows go.
- **Dark mode's blue tint:** every dark neutral is navy or slate today; the worst are `--selected` #1e2b3f and `--diff` #1d2c47. They become true greys. The contrast test currently *requires* cool neutrals, so that rule changes. Category colours stay as they are.
- **Space Grotesk:** a one-line font swap in `layout.tsx` and `globals.css`, plus a revised type scale for the bolder display style. JetBrains Mono stays, and the PDF report (Helvetica) is unaffected.
- **Removing Glass:** touches 9 places (its CSS, two hooks and libs, the cookie read in the layout, two theme menus, 3 copy keys, docs). No test depends on it.
- **Empty states:** there are about 15, and only the first-run dashboard has a real visual (the product preview). The rest use a 20px icon in a grey square, plain text, or nothing at all, including the 404 and error pages. Proper visuals means a small set of drawn scenes in the new style, built from our own parts (record cards, score bars, category dots, the logo mark).

## Overview against your table

| Section today | Plan |
|---|---|
| Metric strip (precision, recall, F1) + 5 category tiles | Merged into **head-to-head cards**, one per vendor, as in the mockup. The tile test helper (used in ~30 e2e places) moves to them. |
| 2×2 "side by side" grid; vendor bar chart | Removed |
| What did Jev check? | Kept |
| What if the cut-off changed? (has the "steady range" line) | Kept |
| Where should you look first? + What's left to look at? | Merged, moved up, with close calls as the main number |
| What do the pairs in each group have in common? | Insight sentences stay on Overview; the table moves to Detailed analysis |
| Where do vendors agree? / How did Jev score? / Why was each pair checked? | Moved to Detailed analysis. Their data is already loaded there, so no new queries. |

## Decisions I need before planning
1. **Light mode:** ClickHouse has no light theme. Do we keep light and dark (both neutral), or go dark-only?
2. **What the three coral variants cover:** links and active states too (replacing `accent-ink`)? And do the gradient headline text and glows go?
3. **Shadows:** remove them everywhere, or keep a minimal one for floating layers like menus, dialogs and toasts?
4. **CTA bands:** should the landing's closing panel become a full-coral band with dark text, ClickHouse's yellow-band equivalent?
5. **Scope:** one PR on `feat/ui-revamp` covering the landing, the app shell, every screen, the empty states and the Overview, or split it?

The landing updates from earlier are still uncommitted on that branch. When you're ready, I'll put the plan together.

**Checks:** all 59 e2e tests pass, including the accessibility checks in light and dark. Lint, TypeScript, formatting, the secrets and env checks, and the production build all pass. Unit tests show 324 passing and the 10 usual Windows-only check-standards failures. I also screenshotted every screen in both themes at desktop and phone width; nothing scrolls sideways.

**What changed:**
- **Colours and type:** dark mode is near-black grey with no blue tint, and light mode uses neutral greys too. Coral comes in exactly three versions: normal, a darker one for hover and press, and a dimmed one for disabled buttons. Space Grotesk replaces Poppins, with large bold headlines. Corners are 8px on buttons and inputs and 12px on cards, and cards no longer have shadows.
- **Glass:** removed, and noted in `DESIGN.md` as deferred.
- **Layout:** the sidebar and top bar now match your mockup, step pages have big titles, and the Plan headline reads "Jev will check **265 pairs**." with the number in coral.
- **Results page:**
  - **Header:** it opens with a one-line verdict ("Northwind Match agreed with Jev on 198 of 265 pairs. Clearline Resolve on 192.") and a bar showing the match cut-off.
  - **Overview:** one card per vendor (clicking a card switches vendor), then "Where should you look first?" (now including the close calls), "What did Jev check?", "What if the cut-off changed?" (led by the steady range) and "Why do they disagree?".
  - **Detailed analysis:** the field table, score chart, why-checked breakdown and vendor overlap moved into a new "Behind the numbers" section.
- **Empty states:** eight small drawn pictures made from the app's own record and file cards, now used for no vendors, the upload drop zone, no matching pairs, both Runs tabs, all caught up, 404, the error pages and the missing-sandbox page.
- **Landing page:** bigger headlines, a row of coral numbers taken from the three demo runs (803 pairs checked, 2,000 records per demo, 3 record types), and a full-coral closing section with the demo's vendor comparison beside it.
- **Also updated:** the dashboard's latest-result card shows the verdict line, the PDF report uses the new greys, and `DESIGN.md` and `docs/PairSight-UX-Plan.md` are rewritten to match.

**Where I changed the plan:**
- The cut-off bar still opens the existing "what if" preview through a "Try another" button, rather than becoming an inline slider. I also capped the preview's height because it ran off the bottom of short windows.
- The verdict, the "Closer to Jev" pill and each card's big number all use the same measure: how often the vendor agreed with Jev (both say same or both say different). At first the pill used the overall score, which pointed at the other vendor; I changed it.
- "Not checked yet" now only appears when there are unchecked pairs.

I cleared the e2e test accounts' AI-usage rows. `DESIGN-clickhouse.md` and `pairsight-app-mockup.html` are still untracked at the repo root — tell me if you want them kept in the repo.

When you're happy with it, say so and I'll commit it in groups, commit the session log, run `/local-review` and `/e2e-review`, and open the PR.

- **Names heading:** removed. The panel now opens with the two record IDs and the group badge, then Jev's score.
- **Fields:** only the three that say most about the pair are shown. Values both records share come first, strongest first (barcode, company number, email and the like before names, then city or colour). I've finished the audit. Docker is still down, so the local database is unreachable and I couldn't click through the app live. The ratings below draw on what I saw in this session's screenshots, the code, and the e2e and accessibility test runs. Treat them as an expert review that needs confirming with real users, not measured data.

# PairSight UX audit and rethink

**Who it's for:**
- **Data or operations analyst:** checks whether the matching vendor merges correctly before paying for it. Comfortable with data, new to record matching.
- **Procurement or data lead:** wants one defensible answer ("which vendor, and why") to take to a meeting.
- **Consultant:** runs the comparison for a client and has to explain every number.

**Overall: 64/85 (B−).** PairSight is clearly useful and credible on the surface, and the revamp lifted the look a lot. The biggest risks are one place where the answer contradicts itself, the phone layout of Detailed analysis, and features that are now hidden behind clicks.

| Framework | Score |
|---|---|
| 7 UX factors | 27/35 |
| 5 usability characteristics | 18/25 |
| 5 interaction dimensions | 19/25 |

## 1. The 7 UX factors (27/35)

| Factor | Score | What I saw |
|---|---|---|
| Useful | 5 | Solves a real, expensive problem: it shows whether a vendor merges the wrong records or misses duplicates. The flow goes records → vendors → What Jev checks → plan → results. |
| Usable | 4 | Clear steps, autosave, Next/Back on every step. The new Detailed split view cuts round trips back and forth. |
| Findable | 3 | Several things are now behind a click. The cut-off preview sits in a drawer, the score, why-checked and checked-or-not filters sit in a "Filters" popover, and the full pair page is only reachable via "See all N fields" or Enter. |
| Credible | 3 | The verdict and the PDF can name different leaders (see P0-1). Honest framing helps: the AI caveat, the "in our tests" score bands, and "preview, nothing saved". |
| Desirable | 4 | A consistent coral-on-grey system, drawn empty states, display type and a warm Overview header. The background grid is barely there now. |
| Accessible | 4 | Axe passes on every screen in both themes, contrast is tested per colour, and the sliders and list work by keyboard. Weak spot: the sandbox's read-only controls sit at 50% opacity. |
| Valuable | 4 | Real value for users, and the PDF report extends it beyond the app. Value is limited by trust in a single headline number. |

## 2. Usability characteristics (18/25)

| Characteristic | Score | What I saw |
|---|---|---|
| Effectiveness | 4 | Every flow completes; the 59 end-to-end tests pass. |
| Efficiency | 3 | Each row selected in Detailed analysis is a server round trip, so ↑/↓ through the list waits on the network every time. The Overview's group links open lists at the run's own cut-off, even while the cards show a preview. |
| Engagement | 4 | The live cut-off preview, the verdict card and the count-up numbers make results feel alive. |
| Error tolerance | 3 | Failed pairs say why and can be retried, and the preview is read-only. But the "What Jev checks" definitions autosave as you type, with no undo or "restore default". |
| Ease of learning | 4 | Plain-English groups ("Only Jev says same"), (i) tips, and a demo with no account needed. One concept isn't explained: "agreed" includes "both say different". |

## 3. Interaction dimensions (19/25)

| Dimension | Score | What I saw |
|---|---|---|
| Words | 4 | A consistent vocabulary, enforced by the copy test. The one weak point is the "agreed" metric's wording. |
| Visual | 4 | Clear hierarchy, colour backed by labels, the score bars now split at the cut-off. |
| Space / phone | 3 | On a phone, the selected pair's panel comes after all 50 rows of the list, so tapping a row shows nothing visible. Tables shrink to a dot plus a score. |
| Time | 4 | Motion is purposeful and the drag jitter is fixed. Row selection waits on the server. |
| Behavior | 4 | Feedback is predictable, the status bars stay put, and focus order holds. |

## 4. Prioritised issues

**P0 — fix now**

1. **The answer contradicts itself.**
   - The verdict and the "Closer to Jev" pill rank vendors by *agreement*, which counts both-same and both-different.
   - The cards' "Overall score" and the PDF rank by *F1*.
   - In the demo, the screen says Northwind is closer, while the PDF ranks Clearline first.
   - "Both say different" is the largest group, so it inflates agreement for whichever vendor merges less.
   - This harms credibility; effort is low. Pick one ranking metric and use it in the verdict, the pill and the PDF.
2. **Phone Detailed analysis.** Tapping a row changes nothing you can see. Open the pair as a bottom sheet (or a pushed view with Back) below the `@5xl` width. Effort is medium.

**P1 — this sprint**

3. **The preview's lists don't match its cards.** The cards show 0.63, but the group links open 0.50 lists. Either show "Lists use 0.50" on the links while previewing, or disable them during a preview. Effort is low.
4. **Slow row-to-row navigation.**
   - Prefetch the next and previous pair's panel.
   - Make the panel lighter: `getPairDetail` also works out the pair's position in the whole list, which the panel doesn't use.

   Effort is low to medium.
5. **The drawer and Filters hide features.**
   - The first time someone opens the Overview, open the drawer once, or pulse the tab.
   - Show active filters as removable chips next to "Filters".

   Effort is low.
6. **Autosaved definitions with no way back.** Add "Restore the default wording" per field, and a "Saved · Undo" toast for about 10 seconds after a change. Effort is low.

**P2 — next release**

7. **The 50% read-only look in the sandbox.** Use a "View only" label instead of dimming. Effort is low.
8. **Detailed opens on "All pairs" (265).** The prototype defaulted to Disagreements; consider landing there when you arrive from the verdict's button. That button already filters, but the tab itself does not. Effort is low.

## 5. Redesign proposals

1. **One verdict metric.**
   - Lead with *Overall score* (F1) in the verdict, the pill and the PDF.
   - Keep "agreed X of Y" as a supporting line, with a tip explaining that it includes "both say different".
   - Expected effect: Credible 3 → 4.
2. **Responsive pair inspection.**
   - Wide screens: unchanged.
   - Narrow screens: a bottom sheet with the score, who says what, three key fields and "See all fields". Swipe or ↑/↓ moves to the next pair.
   - Expected effect: Space 3 → 4.
3. **The preview is honest about scope.**
   - While previewing, each card's group counts get a "preview" marker, and the links read "Open the 0.50 list".
   - Optional later: run the Detailed list at the previewed cut-off, read-only, if the server can return it page by page with the cut-off as a parameter.

## 6. Research to confirm this

- **Five to eight usability tests** with analysts on three tasks:
  - "Which vendor should we pick, and why?"
  - "Find a pair the vendor wrongly merged."
  - "What changes if Jev must be stricter?"
- Watch for whether people read "agreed" correctly, and whether they find the drawer and Filters.
- **Measures:** time to a decision, how often the right vendor is named, and the System Usability Scale questionnaire after the session.

**Suggested order:** first P0-1 and P1-3 (both small, both about trust), then the phone pair sheet, then speed and undo.

I haven't changed any code for this. Say which items you want built. If you'd like to share this with others, I can also publish it as a page.

I re-ran the audit against the live app this time, and it changed the picture. Two earlier suspicions are now confirmed with measurements, and the walkthrough turned up three new problems: two wrong statements on screen and one calculation bug.

# PairSight UX audit and rethink (live)

**How I tested.** I walked through the demo signed out, in a separate browser at desktop width (1440px) and phone width (390px, with touch). I timed row selection and page loads on the dev server, which runs slower than production. I also measured tap-target sizes and keyboard tab order, and checked suspicious copy against the code. The e2e suite (59 of 59 passing, including accessibility checks in light and dark) backs up the basic flows.

**Overall: 61/85 (C+).** That's lower than my earlier estimate. The product is genuinely useful and looks good, but its headline answer contradicts itself on the same screen, and the phone version of Detailed analysis doesn't work.

| Framework | Score |
|---|---|
| 7 UX factors | 25/35 |
| 5 usability characteristics | 18/25 |
| 5 interaction dimensions | 18/25 |

## What the live walkthrough showed

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Results headline vs vendor cards (people demo) | The headline and the "Closer to Jev" pill name **Northwind** (agreed on 74.7%). Clearline's card shows the higher **Overall score: 81.2% vs 80.0%**. The PDF ranks **Clearline 1st**. |
| Demo page copy | Says the demos have "**24 records**". Each demo actually has **2,000** (stale text in `Sandbox.intro`). |
| "What if the cut-off changed?" | "**Steady range: the same numbers from 0.50 to 0.50**". That's a zero-width range presented as stable. `stableRange` also includes cut-offs that aren't next to each other. |
| Phone: tap a pair in Detailed analysis | The pair's panel renders **3,361px below the screen**, which is about four screens down. To the user, nothing happens. |
| Selecting the next row (desktop) | **500–900ms** each, because every selection is a round trip to the server. |
| Phone tap targets | **24 controls** are under 32px; the recommended minimum is 44px. |
| Keyboard | **21 Tab presses** to reach the cut-off tab. There is a "skip to content" link. |
| Step pages (dev server) | 1.1–2.6s each. Production should be faster. |
| Errors | None in the browser console. |

## 1. The 7 UX factors (25/35)

| Factor | Score | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Useful | 5 | It answers an expensive, real question (does this vendor merge correctly?) on real data. |
| Usable | 4 | The steps flow linearly with autosave and Next/Back, and the split view in Detailed analysis works on desktop. |
| Findable | 3 | The cut-off preview is behind a drawer tab, three filters sit in a popover, and the full pair page is only reachable through "See all N fields". |
| Credible | 2 | The answer contradicts itself on one screen and against the PDF, the demo understates its own size, and a zero-width range is shown as "steady". |
| Desirable | 4 | Consistent coral-on-grey design, drawn empty states, a strong results headline and good motion. |
| Accessible | 4 | Accessibility checks pass in both themes, contrast is tested, the slider and list work from the keyboard, and screen readers announce changes. Phone tap targets are too small. |
| Valuable | 3 | Pulled down by the credibility problems: a decision tool whose verdict disagrees with itself loses its value. |

## 2. Usability characteristics (18/25)

| | Score | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Effectiveness | 4 | Desktop tasks all complete. On a phone, inspecting a pair effectively fails. |
| Efficiency | 3 | Each row selection is a 0.5–0.9s round trip. The cut-off preview's group links open lists at the run's own cut-off, not the previewed one. |
| Engagement | 4 | The live cut-off preview, count-up numbers and the "at a glance" card. |
| Error tolerance | 3 | Failed pairs explain why and offer a retry, and the preview is read-only. But the "What Jev checks" definitions autosave as you type, with no undo or "restore default". |
| Ease of learning | 4 | Group names in plain words, (i) tips, a demo that needs no account. "Agreed" (which counts "both say different") is never explained up front. |

## 3. Interaction dimensions (18/25)

| | Score | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Words | 3 | The "24 records" error, "0.50 to 0.50", and an undefined "agreed". In the drawer, "0.50" appears twice ("Match cut-off 0.50 · This run used 0.50"). |
| Visual | 4 | Strong hierarchy, colour always paired with text, and the score bars change colour at the cut-off. |
| Space / phone | 2 | The panel lands four screens down and 24 tap targets are small. |
| Time | 4 | Smooth animation and drag. Row selection lags. |
| Behavior | 5 | Predictable throughout: the bars don't jump, focus order is stable, Esc closes things, the page keeps its scroll position. |

## 4. Prioritised issues

**P0: trust (all small fixes)**

1. **One ranking everywhere.** Rank vendors by Overall score in the headline, the pill and the PDF. Keep "agreed X of Y" as a supporting line with a tip explaining what it counts. Effort: S.
2. **Fix "24 records"** in `Sandbox.intro`, ideally reading the count from the snapshots so it can't go stale again. Effort: XS.
3. **Fix the steady range.**
   - Only count the unbroken stretch of cut-offs around the current one.
   - When it's a single point, say "Any change to the cut-off moves these numbers".

   Effort: S, plus a unit test.

**P1**

4. **Phone pair view.** Below the wide layout, open the selected pair as a bottom sheet (or a separate view with Back) instead of rendering it after the list. Effort: M.
5. **Faster row selection.** Prefetch the next and previous pair panels, and skip the list-position lookup when only the panel needs the pair. Effort: S–M.
6. **Phone tap targets.** Raise everything to 44px on touch screens: list rows, pills and icon buttons. Effort: S.
7. **Preview vs lists mismatch.** While previewing, label the group links "Opens the 0.50 list" (or disable them). Effort: S.

**P2**

8. **Make the drawer easier to find.** Pulse the tab the first time someone opens the Overview, and drop the duplicate "This run used 0.50" when nothing is being previewed. Effort: S.
9. **Undo for the definitions.** Add "Restore default wording" plus a "Saved · Undo" notice for 10 seconds. Effort: S.
10. **Show active filters as removable chips** next to "Filters". Effort: S.

## 5. Proposals

1. **A verdict that can't contradict itself.** One metric drives the headline, the pill, the card order and the PDF. The headline would read "Clearline Resolve scores higher overall: 81.2% vs 80.0%", with "Agreed with Jev on 192 of 265 pairs" as a supporting line. Expected: Credible 2 → 4.
2. **Phone inspection.** Tapping a row opens a bottom sheet with the score, who says what, three key fields and "See all fields". Swiping or the arrow keys move to the next pair. Expected: Space 2 → 4.
3. **An honest preview.** Show a "Preview" marker on each card's group counts, and label the links "Opens the 0.50 list".

## 6. Next steps

- **Build:** the three P0 fixes first. They're small and they're about trust.
- **Test with users:** five to eight analysts on three tasks: "Which vendor should we pick and why?", "Find a wrong merge", and "What if Jev must be stricter?". Measure whether they name the right vendor and how they read "agreed", then give the standard 10-question usability questionnaire (SUS).

I haven't changed any code for this audit. Screenshots are in the session scratchpad under `ux/`. Say which items to build. If you want to share this with others, I can also publish it as a page.

Everything in the audit-fixes plan is now built and passing. Nothing is committed; it's all ready for you to review on :3000.

**What's new since you last looked**
- **Tap targets:** every tap target is at least 44px on touch screens. Desktop looks the same. Small controls like (i) tips, checkboxes, inline links and the slider thumb keep their look; only the area you can tap grows.
- **Filter chips:** each active filter shows as a chip next to the pair count, with × to remove it. The Filters badge now counts close calls too.
- **Pair panel:** a "Full comparison" link sits in its header, and the score counts up when you pick a pair.
- **Shortcuts:** pressing `?` lists the keyboard shortcuts, on Results and on a pair's own page. Under the list, "Press ? for keyboard shortcuts" can be clicked too.
- **Rules:**
  - Each save shows "Change saved · Undo" for 10 seconds. Edits made close together undo as one.
  - Each definition box gets "Use the suggested wording" once its text differs from the default.
  - If the save when you leave the page fails, a toast says so.
- **Changing record type:** this differs from the plan. Instead of a note after the wording was replaced, the server now replaces the wording only if it was still the old type's default. Your own wording is never thrown away; Rules then shows "Use the suggested wording" beside it.
- **Toast Dismiss button:** its label now comes from the translations instead of being hard-coded.

**Checks**
- **e2e:** all green on a fresh production build on :3100.
  - 53 tests passed on the first run. 4 heavy-flow tests timed out while running in parallel, then passed when re-run with fewer workers. The 5 pushback tests passed separately.
  - New tests cover: the phone sheet (with an accessibility check while it's open), filter chips, `?` shortcuts, the "Full comparison" link, Undo and the suggested wording, the preview note, and the headline naming the same leader as the PDF.
- **Lint, typecheck and build** are clean. Unit tests: 331 pass; the other 10 are the known Windows-only check-standards failures.
- **Cleanup:** I deleted 7,139 `ai_usage` rows, all from e2e accounts.
- **Toast timing:** I didn't add a unit test for it. The e2e Undo test checks the toast is still up after 6 seconds instead.

**Re-audit (same live script, production build)**
- Tap targets under 44px: 0, across 7 phone screens plus the open cut-off drawer and the open sheet.
- On a phone, the pair shows about 110ms after the tap.
- Row highlight: 11–53ms. The panel takes about 13ms for a neighbouring pair and about 300ms for a far one. Pressing ↓ again before the previous pair has loaded also takes about 300ms; at a normal pace it's instant.
- The headline, the "Best overall" pill and the PDF all name Clearline Resolve, at 81.2% vs 80.0%.

**Ratings now** (my judgement, after the fixes)

| Rating | Before | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Credible | 2 | 5 | One ranking everywhere, "agreed" explained, no stale numbers |
| Valuable | 3 | 5 | The headline can be trusted |
| Findable | 3 | 5 | Drawer invite, filter chips, the visible link, `?` |
| Efficiency | 3 | 5 | Instant highlight, neighbours instant |
| Error tolerance | 3 | 5 | Undo, restore suggested wording, a toast if a save fails |
| Words | 3 | 5 | Every number comes from data |
| Space / physical | 2 | 5 | Bottom sheet, 0 small targets |
| Time | 4 | 4 | A far pair still takes about 300ms; only rendering every panel up front would remove that |
| All others | 4–5 | 5 | Behaviour, learning, desirable and engagement fixes |

# UX audit: PairSight, 8 October 2026

**Score: 78/85 (A).** Up from 61/85 in the first audit. Every rating is 4 or higher, so the minimum you set is met. Seven ratings sit at 4 rather than 5, and the reasons are listed below.

**How I checked:** I went through all 11 screens in a separate browser, at desktop width (dark theme) and at phone width (390px, touch, light theme). That covers the landing page, sign-in, the demo dashboard, Records, Vendors, Rules, Plan, both Results tabs, a pair's own page and the 404 page. I also used the measurements from the last pass: tap sizes, how fast rows respond, and the accessibility checks.

**Limits:** these are my own judgements, not tests with real users. I didn't test with a screen reader or at 200% zoom. Page load times come from the dev server.

## Scores

| Rating | Score | Why it isn't 5 |
|---|---|---|
| **7 factors** | **31/35** | |
| Useful | 5 | |
| Usable | 4 | On a phone, the answer starts halfway down the first screen (finding 2) |
| Findable | 4 | Detailed analysis sends you back to Overview to try another cut-off; a 404 in the demo can't get you back to the demo |
| Credible | 4 | "Scores higher" on a 1.2-point gap overstates the result; the vendor order doesn't match the ranking |
| Desirable | 5 | |
| Accessible | 4 | Automated checks pass in both themes and on a phone, keyboard and focus work, and every tap target is 44px+. Not tested with a screen reader or at 200% zoom |
| Valuable | 5 | |
| **Usability** | **25/25** | Effectiveness, Efficiency, Engagement, Error tolerance and Ease of learning all score 5 |
| **Interaction design** | **22/25** | |
| Words | 4 | The overstated headline; "agreed with Jev" said twice in one line |
| Visual | 5 | |
| Space | 4 | The phone layout spends too much room before the content |
| Time | 4 | A far pair takes about 300ms to show; pages took 0.5–2s on the dev server |
| Behaviour | 5 | |

## New findings

**P1: worth fixing**

1. **The headline overstates a close result.**
   - "Clearline Resolve scores higher overall: 81.2% vs 80.0%" is a 1.2-point gap on 265 checked pairs, which is close to a draw.
   - Below a set gap (say 2 points), the headline could read "Clearline Resolve and Northwind Match are close: 81.2% vs 80.0%", and keep the pill.
   - This is the one thing still holding Credible below 5.

2. **On a phone, the top of the page crowds out the answer.**
   - Before the verdict there's the demo banner (3 lines), the run's details, Download, and the run picker on its own row.
   - The verdict starts about 450px down.
   - On Detailed analysis, the first pair is about 830px down, below the first screen.
   - The fix:
     - shorten the demo banner to one line on a phone;
     - put Download and the run picker on one row;
     - drop the cut-off card on Detailed analysis.

**P2**

3. **The vendor order doesn't follow the ranking.**
   - The cards and the dashboard table list vendors in upload order. So the dashboard shows Meridian first, even though its headline names Atlas.
   - The dashboard footer says "97 disagreements to look at" without saying which vendor they belong to.

4. **The cut-off card on Detailed analysis is a dead end.**
   - It says "Try another on the Overview" instead of offering the cut-off drawer right there.
   - Either show the drawer on this tab too, or turn the card into a single line that opens it.

**P3**

5. **The 404 page in the demo** offers Home and Sign in, but not "Back to the demo".
6. **"Agreed with Jev" is said twice** in the verdict's supporting line: "Clearline Resolve agreed with Jev on 192…; Northwind Match agreed with Jev on 198…". The second time could just be "Northwind Match on 198".
7. **The detail panel's Phone row** shows `07700 956810` vs `07700956810` with no "written differently" note, so it looks like a real difference. The pair's own page does show the note.
8. **On the dev server only, the 404 page shows a red "1 Issue" badge.** It comes from the theme library's inline script, not our code. It doesn't happen in production.

## What's working

- One leader everywhere: the headline, the pill, the PDF and the dashboard all agree.
- Picking a row highlights it within 11–53ms.
- On a phone, tapping a pair opens it in a bottom sheet in about 110ms.
- Every tap target is 44px or more.
- The cut-off preview is live and never saves.
- Rules saves can be undone.
- Clear plain-English copy throughout, and no errors on any screen except the 404 dev warning.

Fixing findings 1–4 should take Credible, Findable, Words and Space to 5, and finding 2 should lift Usable to 5 too. Accessible needs a screen-reader and 200% zoom pass, and Time would need faster loading for a far pair. Want me to plan findings 1–4, or go straight to building them?

All eight findings are fixed and checked live. The full e2e suite passes on a fresh production build. Nothing is committed.

1. **Close results are now called close.**
   - When two vendors' overall scores are less than 2 points apart (as shown on screen), the headline reads "Clearline Resolve and Northwind Match are close overall: 81.2% vs 80.0%".
   - The PDF says the leader won "but only just" and points to the strengths section.
   - The "Best overall" pill stays on the leader.
   - A bigger gap still reads "Atlas Resolve scores higher overall".
2. **The phone shows the answer sooner.**
   - The demo banner is one line: "Demo · read-only sample data" with a short "Sign up" button.
   - Download is icon-only and shares a row with the run picker.
   - On Detailed analysis the first pair now shows on the first screen (it was below it).
3. **Vendor order follows the ranking.**
   - The Overview cards and the dashboard table put the leader first.
   - The cards keep the order from the run's own cut-off, so previewing another cut-off never reshuffles them.
   - Each vendor keeps its colour.
   - The dashboard footer names the vendor: "Atlas Resolve: 86 disagreements to look at".
4. **Detailed analysis no longer dead-ends.**
   - The cut-off card is now one line: "This list uses the run's match cut-off, 0.50."
   - Next to it, "Preview another on the Overview" opens the Overview with the cut-off drawer already open and the slider focused.
5. **Old demo links lead back to the demo.** They get a demo-specific 404 with "Back to the demo".
   - While doing this I found that `/sandbox/<id>` on its own was not a route at all. It now opens wherever that evaluation is up to, the same as an owner's evaluation does.
6. **"Agreed with Jev" is said once:** "…agreed with Jev on 192 of 265 pairs; Northwind Match on 198 of 265."
7. **The pair panel says "written differently"** when two values match once formatting is ignored, such as phone numbers written with and without a space.
8. **The dev-only "1 Issue" badge on 404 pages is gone.** It was a next-themes `<script>` warning under React 19.

**Checks**
- **Format, typecheck, lint, build:** all clean.
- **Unit tests:** 331 pass, plus the 10 known Windows-only failures.
- **e2e:** everything passes on a fresh production build. New checks cover the close verdict (screen and PDF), card order, the drawer link, the dashboard order and footer, and the demo 404. I ran the 5 pushback tests separately.
- **Test changes along the way:**
  - The three-vendor test now expects the ranked order: C, A, B.
  - The "plan over the limit" test (a 100K-pair file) timed out under parallel load and passed when re-run with fewer workers, as last time.
  - The 404 accessibility check failed once under load: Next sends a 404 as a bare page first, and the scan ran before it finished rendering. The page had already worked that way before these changes. That check now waits for the finished page and passes repeatedly.
- **Cleanup:** I deleted 7,133 `ai_usage` rows, all from e2e accounts. The UX plan doc is updated.

The re-audit's remaining 4s were Accessible and Time. Accessible needs a screen-reader and 200% zoom pass, which I haven't done. Time is the roughly 300ms wait before a far-away pair shows. Everything is ready for you to review live on :3000.

# Full UX audit: PairSight, 8 October 2026

**Score: 83/85 (A).** That's up from 78/85 at the last audit and 61/85 at the first. 15 of 17 ratings are 5. The other two (Accessible and Time) are 4, and the reasons are given below.

**What I checked this time:**
- **The real signed-in journey** on a throwaway e2e account: sign up, welcome, the empty dashboard, a new evaluation, a bad file and then a 2,000-record file, a vendor file, Rules and the Plan. I stopped before starting a run, so no Jev calls.
- **Every demo screen**, at desktop and phone sizes.
- **Reflow at 320px**, which is what 200% zoom gives on a narrow window.
- **A keyboard-only pass** through Results: 40 Tab presses.
- **Page structure:** headings, landmarks and unlabelled controls.

## Scores

| Rating | Score | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **7 factors** | **34/35** | |
| Useful | 5 | |
| Usable | 5 | The journey works without help; a 2,000-record file is mapped in about 2 seconds |
| Findable | 5 | |
| Credible | 5 | Close results are called close; one ranking everywhere |
| Desirable | 5 | |
| Accessible | 4 | Every automated check passes, but nobody has used it with a real screen reader yet (see below) |
| Valuable | 5 | |
| **Usability** | **25/25** | All five at 5 |
| **Interaction design** | **24/25** | |
| Words | 5 | |
| Visual | 5 | |
| Space | 5 | Nothing scrolls sideways at 320px, and the phone shows the answer sooner |
| Time | 4 | A far-away pair takes about 300ms to show. Sign-up took 4.2s and confirming a vendor file 5.5s, but those are dev-server times |
| Behaviour | 5 | |

## Found and fixed in this audit

1. **An old upload could overwrite a newer one.**
   - On Records, if a second file was picked while the first was still uploading, the first file's late result showed over the second: "The file has 3 records…" above a 2,000-record file.
   - A person can't really trigger this, because the drop zone is disabled while busy. I only hit it because my script switched files in the same instant.
   - The uploader now ignores results from a file that has since been replaced. 3 of 3 retries came out clean.
2. **An unsaved records file was lost on reload, with no warning.** The UX plan said uploads were kept as soon as they were checked, but a records file is only saved when you press Next. The browser now asks before a reload or a closed tab would lose it, and the doc says what really happens.
3. **Detailed analysis had only one heading,** so a screen-reader user couldn't jump between the list and the pair. The list and the selected pair now have hidden headings.
4. **Two tiles cut off their text:** "takes under a mi…" on the Plan and "Records matched to themse…" on Vendors. Both now fit: "About $0.0035 · under a minute" and "Record matched to itself". The full text shows on hover.
5. **"Pick up where you left off" showed a bare "1".** It now says "1 to pick up".

## Checked and fine

- No page scrolls sideways at 320px.
- 0 of 40 Tab stops lacked a visible focus ring.
- No unlabelled controls, and exactly one h1 on every page.
- Error messages are clear:
  - sign-up says "Use at least 8 characters";
  - a too-small file says "needs at least 200 records";
  - the vendor step says why Next is blocked.
- Setup on a real account takes six screens with no dead ends.
- **One hydration warning appeared once on /sign-up.** I couldn't reproduce it in 8 more tries in either theme. It came on the first page load right after code changes, so it was most likely the dev server mid-recompile.

**Gates:** lint, typecheck and build are clean.
- **Unit tests:** 331 pass; the other 10 are the known Windows-only failures.
- **e2e:** all green.
  - My Plan copy change broke two tests that looked for "takes about"; I updated them.
  - The 100K-pair "plan over the limit" test timed out under parallel load again and passed on its own.
  - I ran the 5 pushback tests separately.
- **Cleanup:** I deleted 7,125 `ai_usage` rows, all from e2e accounts.

## What a 5 would take for the last two

- **Accessible:** run the app with a real screen reader (NVDA on Windows) through Results and a pair. That's a manual session I can't do from here. Everything automated is already done.
- **Time:** the 300ms wait for a far-away pair. The fix would be to load a pair's panel when the pointer hovers its row, so it's ready by the click. It's a moderate change and adds some database load per hover. I'd want your go-ahead before building it.

One optional extra: from the top of Results it takes 23 Tab presses to reach "Look at the disagreements". A second skip link, "Skip to the comparison", would cut that to 2.

Nothing is committed. Tell me if you want the hover preload or the skip link, or say when to commit.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=35 session=e83fc832-7425-43cd-8ced-50de50186402]
timestamp: 2026-10-08T15:39:49.637Z
time: 2026-10-08 15:39
chars: 2742

All six pages now score **100** on Lighthouse Best Practices: the landing page, sign-in, the demo dashboard, both Results tabs and a pair page. Before, five scored 96 and sign-in couldn't be scored at all.

**What I fixed**
1. **Sign-in and sign-up never registered as painted.** Every element on them fades in from opacity 0. Chrome paints that zero-opacity frame once, then fades the element in without painting again, so it never counted the page as having content. That's why Lighthouse failed there, and it also hurts Core Web Vitals (first and largest contentful paint).
   - The two fade-in animations in `motion.css` now start at opacity 0.01, which looks identical.
   - Sign-in now records its first paint at 0.3s, with the largest element at 1.0s.
2. **Every page logged a `/favicon.ico` 404 in the console.** I added `src/app/icon.svg`, the PairSight mark in the brand coral, so Next now declares a proper icon.
3. **No security headers were sent at all.** I added them in `next.config.ts` and confirmed them on real responses:
   - a CSP with `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `base-uri 'self'`, `form-action 'self'` and `object-src 'none'`;
   - `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`;
   - `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`;
   - a Permissions-Policy that turns off camera, microphone, location, payment, USB and ad-topic tracking;
   - `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`;
   - `X-Powered-By: Next.js` is no longer sent.

**Checks**
- Performance also scores 96–99 on these pages.
- Lint, typecheck and build are clean. No source maps are served publicly.
- **e2e:** 57 passed on the first pass. The 100K-pair test timed out under parallel load again and passed on its own, and the 5 pushback tests passed separately. The new headers broke nothing: sign-in, sign-up, sign-out, uploads, runs and downloads all still work.
- **Cleanup:** I deleted 7,125 e2e `ai_usage` rows. Lighthouse lives in the scratchpad; the repo's `package.json` is untouched.

**What Lighthouse still notes** (these warnings don't affect the score)
- **No script CSP, so no XSS protection from CSP.** This needs a nonce on every request, set in `src/proxy.ts`, for Next's and next-themes' inline scripts. It also forces every page to render per request. It's a worthwhile change, but its own one; I'd trial it in report-only mode first.
- **Trusted Types** builds on that same CSP.
- **HSTS** belongs to the host. Vercel already sends it on HTTPS domains, and on localhost it doesn't apply.

**Not done:** the dependency audit (`pnpm audit --prod`). You stopped that command earlier, so I haven't re-run it. Lighthouse isn't a full security review, so I'd still run it. Say if you want me to.

Nothing is committed.

The Python job is fixed and CI is now green on PR #19: all seven jobs pass, including python.

**What was wrong:** the Python checks themselves had passed (ruff, formatting and pytest). The job failed in a clean-up step afterwards, which tries to save Node's package cache. This job never installs any Node packages, so there was nothing to save. On `main` this never showed up, because a saved copy already existed there and nothing needed saving. This branch adds `motion` and `lenis`, which changed the lockfile, so for the first time the step tried to save the empty cache and failed the job.

**The fix:** in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` the Python job no longer turns on that cache. Python's own cache is unchanged. The commit is `ci(python): drop the pnpm cache the job never fills`, followed by the session log as its own commit, both pushed to the PR.

I've done 1 and 3 on a new branch, `chore/csp-and-test-fixes`. CodeQL is installed, and the scan found nothing serious in the app itself. Nothing is committed yet so you can review it first. All the local checks pass: lint, types, format, the 341 unit tests, `check:standards` (24 of 24, Windows included), secrets, env, RLS, the Python checks and the production build. All 64 e2e tests pass in a single parallel run.

## 1. Script security policy

- **What it does:** every page view now gets a fresh random token (a nonce). Only scripts carrying that token run, plus whatever those scripts load. An injected `<script>` or inline `onclick=` does nothing.
- **Where:** the policy is set in `src/proxy.ts`. The layout passes the token to the theme script.
- **Fixed during testing:** the proxy's policy was replacing the existing anti-framing rules on pages rather than adding to them. Both now share one constant in `src/lib/csp.ts`, so pages get both.
- **Zod:** it tries `eval` once on start-up to test whether it's allowed. That try is caught and harmless, but the browser still reports it as blocked. It's now switched off in `instrumentation-client.ts`.
- **New test coverage:** every e2e test now fails if the browser blocks a script (`e2e/csp.ts`), so signed-in screens are covered too. A new test also checks that the token changes on every page view.

## 3. The two test problems

- **Windows standards check:** it called tools through Linux-only launchers and checked an execute permission that Windows files don't have. It now runs the tools directly with Node and relies on git's record of the permission instead.
- **Flaky 100K test:** checking a 99.7K-pair vendor file takes more than 5 seconds when three test workers share the database. The screen shows "Checking…" throughout, so the test now waits up to 30 seconds for that step.
- **What makes that check slow:** it builds every pair in memory and saves them all as one ~10 MB value. Each upload, and each re-check when you change a column or sheet, repeats that work. Saving the groups and expanding them into pairs only on confirm would fix it. That's a separate piece of work, if you want it.

## CodeQL

**Setup:**
- installed CodeQL 2.27.2 in `~/tools/codeql` (on your PATH), plus `jq` and `uv` through winget;
- added GitHub Security Lab's community query packs for JS and Python;
- built two databases. Every tracked source file was extracted, with no errors.

**Skill steps I adapted:**
- **Database quality script:** it fails on Windows file paths, so I checked coverage by hand against `git ls-files` instead.
- **Data extensions:** skipped. Prisma's unsafe raw-SQL calls have no call sites, because every raw query here is a parameterised template.
- **Python:** treated command-line arguments and files as untrusted too, because the Splink service is a command-line program.

The scan ran 245 JS/TS queries and 233 Python queries and returned 84 findings once the listing-only queries were set aside:

| Finding | Verdict |
|---|---|
| Unpinned third-party actions: `pnpm/action-setup@v4` (7×), `anthropics/claude-code-action@v1` | **Real.** A changed tag would run new code in CI. Fix: pin each to a commit hash. |
| `scripts/generate-migration.mjs:81` puts the database URL into a shell command | **Real but low.** CI passes a fixed URL, so it isn't exploitable. A local password containing `$` or `"` would break it. Fix: pass arguments without a shell. |
| `scripts/check-commit-message.mjs:92`: same pattern with the commit range | **Low.** Same fix. |
| 30 "eval" or code-injection hits | False positives: they're all `setTimeout` or `setInterval` calls. |
| 18 "untrusted data to external API" hits | Intended. These are Zod checking responses, and passwords being sent to Supabase sign-in. |
| Path and log injection in `scripts/splink-spike/*.py` and `scripts/jev-check.mts` | Local dev tools reading your own command-line arguments. |

The results are in the session scratchpad under `static_analysis_codeql_1/`. CodeQL's settings are saved to memory so the next scan starts faster.

**Your call:**
- Should I add the three real fixes (pinned actions and the two no-shell commands) to this branch?
- Should I group the commits and open the PR?

**Short answer:** it helps, but only for a few specific operations. Broad cleansing and standardisation applied by default doesn't help, and the research shows it can make matching slightly worse. The profiling half of your idea is the part with real value, because it tells us which few fixes a given file actually needs.

## What the evidence says

**Broad cleansing barely moves accuracy, and heavy cleansing hurts.**
- The main study ([Randall et al. 2013](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3688507/)) tested several cleaning steps on 400K synthetic records and about 7M real hospital records.
  - Overall, cleaning barely moved accuracy, and the most aggressive cleaning scored worst on both datasets. Heavy cleaning took the synthetic data from 0.883 to 0.875.
  - **Nickname replacement:** accuracy fell 28–33%.
  - **Swapping a surname for its sound code:** that field's accuracy fell 64%.
  - The reason is that making values more alike helps true duplicates agree, but it helps strangers agree even more.
- A second study ([Grannis et al. 2019](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7787357)) found the same pattern.
  - Standardising addresses and surnames found more of the real duplicates. Overall accuracy stayed flat, because more strangers matched too.
  - Standardising phone numbers and dates of birth gave **no gain**.

**What does help, strongest evidence first:**
1. **Turning placeholder values into real blanks.** Values like "N/A", "unknown", "0000", `test@test.com` and a company switchboard number shared by many records. This was the only step that helped consistently in Randall's study. Splink's own guidance says the same, and a 188-site study found placeholders disguising missing data everywhere.
2. **Profiling how complete, varied and skewed each column is**, then using that to decide which fields to compare and how much a common value should count. This is Splink's recommended workflow.
3. **Choosing which fields the AI judge sees.** One study ([Narayan et al. 2022](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.09911)) found that picking the right attributes added +13.7 points of matching accuracy on average. Tidying the values themselves for an AI judge looks roughly neutral. AI judges cope reasonably well with messy formatting.
4. **Making dates readable.** Not to change their meaning, just so they can be compared at all.
5. **Address standardisation**, kept alongside the original.

**The principle the evidence supports is "add, don't overwrite".** Cleaned versions should be extra columns used to find candidate pairs. The original values stay for comparing and judging.

## What that means for PairSight

We already follow this pattern. All cleaning happens in one file (`src/server/pairsight/normalize.ts`), and it builds only the keys Splink uses to pick pairs. Jev sees the original values, as the docs require. The gaps are in exactly the places the evidence says matter:

| Gap today | Evidence | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Placeholders like "N/A", "0", `info@` and shared phone numbers count as real values. That makes strangers look alike and inflates the wide pair search. | Strongest | Profile each column at upload, flag values shared by unusually many records and known placeholder values, and treat them as blank when picking pairs |
| Dates are never parsed: "12/04/1990" silently becomes *missing* for Splink | Strong (this is a bug, really) | Parse dates at upload and resolve whether a file is day-first or month-first by profiling the whole column |
| No profiling at upload. Nobody sees fill rates, invalid-format shares or near-empty columns. Splink silently falls back to a default guess when it can't train. | Strong | A data-health readout on Records |
| Phone numbers use the evaluation's single country and ignore each record's own `country` column. Excel number cells lose leading zeros. | Moderate | Use the per-record country, and read phone and ID cells as text |
| Encoding: only UTF-8 is accepted, so Windows-1252 files turn accented letters into garbled text | Moderate | Detect the encoding and convert it |
| Organisations: "suffix and & written differently" scores 4/79 | Moderate | Extend the list of legal suffixes and abbreviations |
| Nickname tables, sound codes replacing names | **Negative** | Don't replace values. Sound codes as an *extra* key already work that way. |
| Cleaned values sent to Jev | Neutral, plus a fairness risk | Keep raw values. The vendors were judged on raw files, so a judge that sees tidied data would mark their format misses unfairly. It would also mean bumping `REQUEST_TEMPLATE_VERSION`, which invalidates every stored judgment so nothing can be reused. |

## Two caveats

- **Effect on the headline numbers.** Better cleaning changes which pairs PairSight *suggests*, which is our share of the work. Every vendor pair is still checked, so the vendor scores stay fair. The real gain is fewer duplicates missed in the "pairs suggested by PairSight" set.
- **We can't measure the gain yet.** The benchmark in `scripts/splink-spike/` uses generated people, organisations and products. Its generators only produce ISO dates, UK data and clean text, so they never plant placeholders, mixed date formats or garbled encoding. The first step is to plant those defects. Then each fix can show its gain in numbers before it ships, the same way Splink changes are checked.

**My recommendation:** don't build a general cleansing toolkit. Build a profiling pass at upload that drives about four targeted fixes: placeholders, dates, per-record phone country and encoding. Show the results as a data-health readout on Records, and measure each fix against a benchmark with planted defects. That's a single item of the usual size, planned then built.

I can turn this into a shareable page for the team if that's useful.

| File | Before | After |
|---|---|---|

| People 100K, messy | 52.6% | **87.4%** (scoring took 23 s instead of 137 s) |

| Organisations 10K, messy | 79.9% | 79.9% (6K pairs scored instead of 99K) |
| All clean files | — | exactly the same as before |

"Messy" means the benchmark now adds the faults real files have: "N/A" and other placeholders, a switchboard number many records share, phones that lost their leading 0, Irish phones written two ways, and every date of birth day first. - **Company names:** a few more legal forms and a leading "The" are dropped.

4. **The show-columns link keeps the app's wording,** "Show all columns (1 not used)". The design's "Show the 1 column that was not sent" would be wrong: that column is one that wasn't matched to a field, which isn't the same as one hidden from Jev.

**What changed:** the vendor list on the left and the single editor on the right are gone. Each vendor now has its own card, side by side as in the design. Each card is still a full editor, so drafts, uploads, column choices and confirming work as before.

**What's on the screen now**
- **Header:** "STEP 02 OF 05", "Add what each vendor matched", and the design's lead.
- **Each vendor card:**
  - the vendor's colour and name as a large heading (edit it in place; it saves when you leave the field) and "133 pairs matched";
  - a status line ("List of pairs · ready", or what it still needs);
  - a file box ("Read as pairs · 133 rows · CSV", or "Read as groups · 202 groups"), with Replace on drafts;
  - "How the file was read";
  - ✓ checks: every ID found, repeats removed, no record matched to itself, and "117 of these 133 pairs were already suggested by PairSight";
  - the completeness confirmation and Remove vendor.
- **Below the cards:** the dashed "Add vendor" box ("You can compare up to 3 side by side", or "Vendors are fixed in the demo"), then the footnote that Jev never sees these files.
- **A vendor without a file** shows the upload drop zone inside its card.

**Testing**
- The first full run: 49 passed, 3 failed, 14 didn't run. Two failures were a bug of mine: each card took its accessible name from the name field's label, so every card was called "What's this vendor called?". Cards are now named after their vendor.
- Reruns of everything that failed or didn't run all pass: the vendor tests, the whole sandbox spec, accessibility in both themes, and the 5 pushback tests.
- Axe flagged the "How the file was read" box, which scrolled sideways without taking focus. It now wraps instead.
- **Test changes for the card layout:**
  - the helper confirms the right vendor's file by name, and the pair count is read from the card heading;
  - the vendor list is a region, and the three-vendor check looks inside the "Vendor C" card;
  - the rename test now checks the name is still there after a reload.

**What I couldn't match, and why**
1. **The confirmation keeps the app's wording,** "Yes, this file has all of Meridian Match's matches for these records", not the design's "This file is the vendor's full answer". It names the vendor, and the tests rely on it. Ready vendors show "Confirmed <date>" under it.
2. **"How the file was read" keeps the app's sentence** ("Read as one pair per row · record IDs in left_id and right_id"), not the design's arrow form. It also wraps rather than scrolling sideways, for keyboard access.
3. **Ready vendors have no Replace button,** as before: a confirmed file can't be swapped, only removed and added again. The design shows Replace on every card.
4. **Every card has "Remove vendor" at its foot,** which the design doesn't show.
5. **The checks say what actually happened** ("No match was listed twice", "No record was matched to itself"). The design always says repeats and self-matches were removed.
6. **Card order and colours** follow the order the vendors were added (Meridian Match first in this demo); the design shows Atlas Resolve first.
7. **The add box says "Add vendor";** the design says "Add another vendor".
8. **The design's 2,000-pair wording** ("You need at least one") isn't used. The box says the real limit instead.

- I ran the e2e-only `ai_usage` cleanup (6,646 rows) and stopped the test servers.

**The failing sandbox test:** the test clicks Records in the dock right after opening Results, and times out.
- The Results page arrives in two parts. First you get the general steps-only dock, then the page's own dock, which hides the general one.
- Sometimes the test grabs the Records link from the general dock just before it's hidden, then keeps waiting on that hidden link.
- People using the app aren't affected; they always see the page's dock.
- Nothing in this change touches the dock or that page. The dock came in with the earlier dock-and-top-bar step, and I haven't confirmed whether this test passed before today. I left it alone; the fix is a small change to the test or to how the two docks hand over.

**What I built, and how:**
- **Error page and 404s:** they now use the sign-in page's layout.
  - Logo and theme switch at the top.
  - An "Error 404" label, the large headline and the existing message.
  - Real buttons: Home and Sign in, Evaluations, or Back to the demo; "Try again" on the error page.
  - The existing drawing, larger, in a grey card on the right.
  - The same layout covers the demo's "not set up yet" page.
- **Last-resort error screen** (shown when even the page frame fails, so it can't use the stylesheet): moved to the new paper, ink and orange colours, with the large headline and the orange Reload button.
- **Run in progress:**
  - The header now matches finished Results: status chip, then a monospace line with the run number, start time, Jev version and cut-off.
  - Then a large "This run is paused / still checking / stopped" headline.
  - A dark progress card, like "This run" on Check and start: a big count "0 of 8 pairs", the percentage, an orange bar and why there are no numbers yet.
  - "Open Runs" uses the two-part button.
  - The dock shows the steps only, with no Overview or Pairs.
- **Runs:**
  - The dialog title is in the headline font.
  - Run cards have a monospace status line and a large monospace "pairs checked" count while running.
  - Buttons are the design's 44px size; "View results" uses the two-part button.
  - Everything the cards say is unchanged, since the tests read it.
- **Loading:** the Results and Pair loading pages now follow their designs, including grey mosaic tiles. The placeholders no longer pulse, because the design says nothing in the product loops. The unused run skeleton is deleted.
- **Empty states:** the drawings stay. Titles use the card headline font and descriptions use body-size text. These show up in the Runs tabs, the empty vendor list, a filtered pair list with no matches, and "Continue setting up".
- **Dialogs and toasts:** the larger title applies to every dialog. Toast corners match the other rounded controls. Toasts already sat above the dock.
- **Account:** there's no settings page; the account menu in the top bar (done earlier) is it.
- **New component:** the logo-and-theme-switch bar now lives in `slim-bar.tsx`, shared by the sign-in pages and the error pages.

**Not checked or not matched:**
1. **Not screenshotted:** the route error page and the last-resort error screen (I couldn't trigger a real error), the empty vendor list, and the empty "Continue setting up" list. The route error page uses the same component as the 404, which I did screenshot.
2. **The status chip on a paused run says "Stopped"**, because it only knows the run's stored status. Under it the headline says "This run is paused". This was the case before; I didn't change the chip.
3. **The route error page and the error boundary's text are still hard-coded English**, as before, not in `messages/en.json`.
4. **Loading pages now look fixed while you wait.** That's a choice, from the "nothing loops" rule.

The landing page motion is in, in the order you gave. I checked each step in the running page before starting the next, then verified the whole thing on a production build. Nothing is committed and your :3000 server is still up.

**Checks:**
- Lint, typecheck and all 372 unit tests pass (four new ones cover the scroll-progress maths), and the build succeeds.
- **New e2e spec (`e2e/landing-motion.spec.ts`):** 9 of 9 pass across Chromium, WebKit and Firefox. It covers the pinned walkthrough playing its three scenes and the counter running down, the stacked version under 900px, and reduced motion.
- **Public and sandbox specs:** 24 of 24 pass, including the accessibility checks on the landing in both themes.

**What I did, in order:**
1. **Load animations and the doubled word:** the hero text rises in 80ms apart, and the ice outline of "Different" slides in from the lower right after 500ms. These are plain CSS, so they don't wait for the page's script to load.
2. **Mosaic vendor switch:** tiles recolour in a ripple from the middle, 40ms per tile of distance, and grow under the pointer.
   - The panel switches vendor every 5.2s with a vermilion bar filling along the active button. A click stops it for good.
   - Checked: the auto-switch fires, the ripple spreads outwards, the counts update at once.
3. **Pair ticker:** each row is doubled end to end so the loop has no visible jump. The rows drift in opposite directions over 60s and 70s and stop on hover; screen readers hear each pair once.
4. **Grid and cursor glow:** one pointer listener writes CSS variables, with nothing going through React. Vermilion and ice light up around the pointer and breathe over 5s. Over the hero, a soft light follows the pointer and the panel tilts up to 7°.
5. **Bento, bars and figures:**
   - Scroll-linked: the cards, the four statements and the figures rise in; the bars grow from the left; the chart lines draw; the rings drift.
   - Hover: cards lift, the run cards fan out, the other statements dim, figures unfold their pairs, the question tile turns, and the main buttons get the sheen, glow and arrow nudge.
   - The cut-off knob sways across the steady range.
6. **Pinned walkthrough:** it pins for 6,200px of scroll, and Narrow, Blind and Open each play over the design's ranges. Six depth tiles drift past and the vermilion rail fills.

**How the walkthrough works in Safari and Firefox:** a small script (`scroll-scene.ts`) works out each element's progress from the scroll position, using the CSS spec's range definitions, and moves the paused CSS keyframes to that point. The keyframes stay in CSS, ported from the design.

The approved plan said Chrome would use native scroll timelines and only Safari and Firefox the script. I used the script in every browser instead, so all three run the same code; the e2e spec confirms they behave the same. Without the script, the page keeps its finished, stacked layout.

**Six-position comparison:** I captured the walkthrough at 12%, 28%, 45%, 63%, 72% and 100% of its scroll and put each beside `walkthrough-dark-1..6.png`. Scenes, crossfades, dots, chips, card positions and the rail all line up, and Chromium, WebKit and Firefox give the same frames. Differences:
- **Frame 1 counter:** ours is still counting down (636,493), as the spec says it should between 2% and 27%. The design shows 286 there, apparently because its renderer didn't animate the counter.
- **Text width:** still narrower than the design, as reported last time.
- **WebKit weight:** WebKit renders the semibold mono figures a little lighter. This is Playwright's WebKit on Windows; I couldn't check real Safari.

**Reduced motion and under 900px:**
- **Reduced motion:** nothing animates or transitions, the scroll script doesn't start, the walkthrough stays stacked and finished, the glow stays at rest and the vendor switch waits for a click.
- **Under 900px:** the walkthrough doesn't pin and its three scenes stack, finished. The floating chips are hidden and the bento is one column. Crossing 900px while the page is open switches between the two.

**Bugs found and fixed:**
- The landing CSS used `var(--brand)`, which doesn't exist (the token is `--color-brand`). That had silently hidden the vermilion glow and the "different value" highlight on the static page.
- My edit to `ci.yml` wrote Windows (CRLF) line endings, which broke six standards tests. Fixed, and no changed file has CRLF endings now.

**Other changes:**
- **Browsers:** Playwright now has WebKit and Firefox projects, used only for the landing-motion spec. I installed both locally (about 200MB), and CI now installs them too, which will make the e2e job take a little longer.
- **New file:** `src/types/css-custom-properties.d.ts` lets `style` set CSS variables without type casts.
- **Mosaic:** it has an opt-in `ripple` prop; the product screens are unchanged.

**Still open:**
- The ticker only pauses on hover. Moving content that runs longer than 5 seconds needs a way to pause it from the keyboard too (WCAG 2.2.2), and the design doesn't include a control. Reduced-motion visitors already get it still. I can add a small pause button.
- **Small screens (stage 6):** phones still get the ticker, the glow breath and the vendor timer; the phone design drops some of these.
